Between the shop running late and the house being so busy, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It keeps things manageable and, yeah, it keeps my weight where I want it.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight gotten so low that a doctor has told you that you are medically underweight for your height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually what got me here. I had to do a work physical, and the doctor there told me my weight is too low. But honestly, to me... I just feel lighter on my feet when I play basketball. And when I'm at the garage, I don't feel as weighed down while I'm working. But yeah, a doctor did tell me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body weight has a big effect on how good or bad you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I don't really look at it like that. My weight doesn't change how I see myself as a person. I'm still just me, you know? A guy working at the shop. \n\nLike I said, I prefer being lighter because it's practical for work and playing basketball, but... no, I wouldn't say it makes me feel like a better or worse guy. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I'm pretty thin, especially since that doctor pointed it out. I don't look in the mirror and see a big guy or anything like that. I just... I see what's there. Like I was saying before, it's really just about how I feel moving around, not... I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re making too much of it. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing at home, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was a real medical danger, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: If you stopped limiting how much you eat, would gaining weight feel unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it would. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tighter, it bothers me. I start thinking I'm getting soft, you know? Like I'm going to be slower moving around the cars at the shop, or I won't be as quick up and down the court when I play ball. So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re making too much of it. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing at home, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was a real medical danger, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: If you stopped limiting how much you eat, would gaining weight feel unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it would. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tighter, it bothers me. I start thinking I'm getting soft, you know? Like I'm going to be slower moving around the cars at the shop, or I won't be as quick up and down the court when I play ball. So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much stick to what I told you—just the coffee and maybe a small sandwich later. I don't really ever have times where I just eat a whole lot of food all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The doctor doing the exam said my weight is too low. \n\nI don't really see it as a problem, honestly. To me, I just feel... lighter on my feet when I play basketball, and I don't feel as weighed down when I'm working under cars at the garage. But, yeah, that's why they told me to come see you.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I am. If I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just worry that if I put on weight, I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at work, or getting up and down the court. So, yeah, I definitely don't want to gain any.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel like you’re overweight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't look in the mirror and think I'm fat right now. It's more just... I see myself as lean, and I want to keep it that way. So I wouldn't say I feel overweight when I look at my body. I just focus on making sure I don't get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up or anything like that. I mostly just... I just watch what I eat and make sure I'm staying active. But no, definitely not that.\n\nDoctor: Do you deliberately eat less than your body needs in order to keep your weight down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I do. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever is around. Between the shop running late and the house being so busy, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It keeps things manageable and, yeah, it keeps my weight where I want it.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight gotten so low that a doctor has told you that you are medically underweight for your height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually what got me here. I had to do a work physical, and the doctor there told me my weight is too low. But honestly, to me... I just feel lighter on my feet when I play basketball. And when I'm at the garage, I don't feel as weighed down while I'm working. But yeah, a doctor did tell me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body weight has a big effect on how good or bad you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I don't really look at it like that. My weight doesn't change how I see myself as a person. I'm still just me, you know? A guy working at the shop. \n\nLike I said, I prefer being lighter because it's practical for work and playing basketball, but... no, I wouldn't say it makes me feel like a better or worse guy. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I'm pretty thin, especially since that doctor pointed it out. I don't look in the mirror and see a big guy or anything like that. I just... I see what's there. I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.1, F50.9, R63.6\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, nice to meet you too. Well, my work physical is actually what got me here. The doctor doing the exam said my weight is too low. \n\nI don't really see it as a problem, honestly. To me, I just feel... lighter on my feet when I play basketball, and I don't feel as weighed down when I'm working under cars at the garage. But, yeah, that's why they told me to come see you.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I am. If I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling tighter, I start thinking I'm getting soft. I just worry that if I put on weight, I'll end up being slower moving around the cars at work, or getting up and down the court. So, yeah, I definitely don't want to gain any.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel like you’re overweight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't look in the mirror and think I'm fat right now. It's more just... I see myself as lean, and I want to keep it that way. So I wouldn't say I feel overweight when I look at my body. I just focus on making sure I don't get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up or anything like that. I mostly just... I just watch what I eat and make sure I'm staying active. But no, definitely not that.\n\nDoctor: Do you deliberately eat less than your body needs in order to keep your weight down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I do. Most days I just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just eat half of whatever is around. Between the shop running late and the house being so busy, I just... I feel better sticking to my own simple rules. It keeps things manageable and, yeah, it keeps my weight where I want it.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight gotten so low that a doctor has told you that you are medically underweight for your height?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's actually what got me here. I had to do a work physical, and the doctor there told me my weight is too low. But honestly, to me... I just feel lighter on my feet when I play basketball. And when I'm at the garage, I don't feel as weighed down while I'm working. But yeah, a doctor did tell me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body weight has a big effect on how good or bad you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I don't really look at it like that. My weight doesn't change how I see myself as a person. I'm still just me, you know? A guy working at the shop. \n\nLike I said, I prefer being lighter because it's practical for work and playing basketball, but... no, I wouldn't say it makes me feel like a better or worse guy. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I'm pretty thin, especially since that doctor pointed it out. I don't look in the mirror and see a big guy or anything like that. I just... I see what's there. Like I was saying before, it's really just about how I feel moving around, not... I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re making too much of it. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing at home, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was a real medical danger, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: If you stopped limiting how much you eat, would gaining weight feel unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it would. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tighter, it bothers me. I start thinking I'm getting soft, you know? Like I'm going to be slower moving around the cars at the shop, or I won't be as quick up and down the court when I play ball. So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much stick to what I told you—just the coffee and maybe a small sandwich later. I don't really ever have times where I just eat a whole lot of food all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise specifically to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the shop, lifting things and moving around, and I play basketball when I can. But I don't... I don't go out and run or hit the gym specifically to burn off calories or keep the weight off. Just the work and the hoops is enough activity for me, really.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you’re thin, does any part of you still feel like your body is too heavy or not lean enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really see it like that. I know I'm thin. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm heavy or anything like that. It's not really about feeling fat... I just like keeping my routine strict, like I said. But no, I know my body isn't heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your body weight or shape is more important to you than it is for most people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't... I don't really care about having a certain shape or hitting a specific number on the scale. It's not about that for me. Like I said before, it's really just about sticking to my routine. I don't spend time worrying about how I look compared to the other guys at the shop or anything. I just do my work. So, no, I wouldn't say it's any more important to me than it is for anyone else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much stick to what I told you—just the coffee and maybe a small sandwich later. I don't really ever have times where I just eat a whole lot of food all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise specifically to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the shop, lifting things and moving around, and I play basketball when I can. But I don't... I don't go out and run or hit the gym specifically to burn off calories or keep the weight off. Just the work and the hoops is enough activity for me, really.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you’re thin, does any part of you still feel like your body is too heavy or not lean enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really see it like that. I know I'm thin. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm heavy or anything like that. It's not really about feeling fat... I just like keeping my routine strict, like I said. But no, I know my body isn't heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your body weight or shape is more important to you than it is for most people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't... I don't really care about having a certain shape or hitting a specific number on the scale. It's not about that for me. Like I said before, it's really just about sticking to my routine. I don't spend time worrying about how I look compared to the other guys at the shop or anything. I just do my work. So, no, I wouldn't say it's any more important to me than it is for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that being this underweight could seriously harm your health?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people have said things like that. I don't really look at it like that. My weight doesn't change how I see myself as a person. I'm still just me, you know? A guy working at the shop. \n\nLike I said, I prefer being lighter because it's practical for work and playing basketball, but... no, I wouldn't say it makes me feel like a better or worse guy. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise specifically to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the shop, lifting things and moving around, and I play basketball when I can. But I don't... I don't go out and run or hit the gym specifically to burn off calories or keep the weight off. Just the work and the hoops is enough activity for me, really.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you’re thin, does any part of you still feel like your body is too heavy or not lean enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really see it like that. I know I'm thin. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm heavy or anything like that. It's not really about feeling fat... I just like keeping my routine strict, like I said. But no, I know my body isn't heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your body weight or shape is more important to you than it is for most people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't... I don't really care about having a certain shape or hitting a specific number on the scale. It's not about that for me. Like I said before, it's really just about sticking to my routine. I don't spend time worrying about how I look compared to the other guys at the shop or anything. I just do my work. So, no, I wouldn't say it's any more important to me than it is for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that being this underweight could seriously harm your health?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people have said things like that. I know they're worried about it. But honestly, I figure they're just making way too much of it. I mean... I still show up for work every day at the shop, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was really harming my health, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I think they're just overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives, diet pills, or similar products to keep your weight down?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use anything like that. But yeah, a doctor did tell me that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body weight has a big effect on how good or bad you feel about yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... I don't really look at it like that. My weight doesn't change how I see myself as a person. I'm still just me, you know? A guy working at the shop. \n\nLike I said, I prefer being lighter because it's practical for work and playing basketball, but... no, I wouldn't say it makes me feel like a better or worse guy. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I'm pretty thin, especially since that doctor pointed it out. I don't look in the mirror and see a big guy or anything like that. I just... I see what's there. Like I was saying before, it's really just about how I feel moving around, not... I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re making too much of it. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing at home, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was a real medical danger, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: If you stopped limiting how much you eat, would gaining weight feel unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it would. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tighter, it bothers me. I start thinking I'm getting soft, you know? Like I'm going to be slower moving around the cars at the shop, or I won't be as quick up and down the court when I play ball. So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much stick to what I told you—just the coffee and maybe a small sandwich later. I don't really ever have times where I just eat a whole lot of food all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise specifically to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the shop, lifting things and moving around, and I play basketball when I can. But I don't... I don't go out and run or hit the gym specifically to burn off calories or keep the weight off. Just the work and the hoops is enough activity for me, really.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you’re thin, does any part of you still feel like your body is too heavy or not lean enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really see it like that. I know I'm thin. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm heavy or anything like that. It's not really about feeling fat... I just like keeping my routine strict, like I said. But no, I know my body isn't heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your body weight or shape is more important to you than it is for most people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't... I don't really care about having a certain shape or hitting a specific number on the scale. It's not about that for me. Like I said before, it's really just about sticking to my routine. I don't spend time worrying about how I look compared to the other guys at the shop or anything. I just do my work. So, no, I wouldn't say it's any more important to me than it is for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that being this underweight could seriously harm your health?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people have said things like that. I know they're worried about it. But honestly, I figure they're just making way too much of it. I mean... I still show up for work every day at the shop, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was really harming my health, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I think they're just overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives, diet pills, or similar products to keep your weight down?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use anything like that. No laxatives, no pills. Like I said, I just stick to my routine with what I eat. I don't need to take anything extra.\n\nDoctor: When you follow your eating rules, does it mainly help you feel in control?\n\nPatient: I don't really think of it like that. It's not about... trying to feel in charge of things or anything. Like I've been saying, it's just my routine. It's just the way I do things every day, so I don't really look at it as a control thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's just about how I physically feel moving around.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your body is bigger than it really is?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I'm pretty thin, especially since that doctor pointed it out. I don't look in the mirror and see a big guy or anything like that. I just... I see what's there. Like I was saying before, it's really just about how I feel moving around, not... I don't think I'm bigger than I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Do you think being this underweight could be medically dangerous for you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, I don't think it's dangerous. I know people are worried about it, but... I figure they’re making too much of it. I mean, I still show up for work at the shop every day, I take care of whatever needs doing at home, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was a real medical danger, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, right?\n\nDoctor: If you stopped limiting how much you eat, would gaining weight feel unacceptable to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it would. I mean, if I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans start feeling a little tighter, it bothers me. I start thinking I'm getting soft, you know? Like I'm going to be slower moving around the cars at the shop, or I won't be as quick up and down the court when I play ball. So yeah, putting on weight isn't really something I'm okay with.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight because you purposely restrict how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess that's fair to say. Most days I really just keep it to coffee in the morning. Then later on, maybe I'll have a small sandwich or just half of whatever's around. With the shop always running late and things being so busy at the house... I don't know, I just feel better sticking to my own simple rules about it. It just works better for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat an unusually large amount of food in a short time and feel unable to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I pretty much stick to what I told you—just the coffee and maybe a small sandwich later. I don't really ever have times where I just eat a whole lot of food all at once or feel like I can't stop. It's usually the opposite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise specifically to prevent gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the shop, lifting things and moving around, and I play basketball when I can. But I don't... I don't go out and run or hit the gym specifically to burn off calories or keep the weight off. Just the work and the hoops is enough activity for me, really.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you’re thin, does any part of you still feel like your body is too heavy or not lean enough?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really see it like that. I know I'm thin. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm heavy or anything like that. It's not really about feeling fat... I just like keeping my routine strict, like I said. But no, I know my body isn't heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your body weight or shape is more important to you than it is for most people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don't... I don't really care about having a certain shape or hitting a specific number on the scale. It's not about that for me. Like I said before, it's really just about sticking to my routine. I don't spend time worrying about how I look compared to the other guys at the shop or anything. I just do my work. So, no, I wouldn't say it's any more important to me than it is for anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that being this underweight could seriously harm your health?\n\nPatient: Yeah, people have said things like that. I know they're worried about it. But honestly, I figure they're just making way too much of it. I mean... I still show up for work every day at the shop, I take care of whatever needs doing around the house, and I still play ball on the weekends. If it was really harming my health, I wouldn't be able to keep up with all that, you know? So I think they're just overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives, diet pills, or similar products to keep your weight down?\n\nPatient: No, I don't use anything like that. No laxatives, no pills. Like I said, I just stick to my routine with what I eat. I don't need to take anything extra.\n\nDoctor: When you follow your eating rules, does it mainly help you feel in control?\n\nPatient: I don't really think of it like that. It's not about... trying to feel in charge of things or anything. Like I've been saying, it's just my routine. It's just the way I do things every day, so I don't really look at it as a control thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel intense fear at the idea of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. It really bothers me. If I see the scale bump up even a pound, or if my jeans feel tighter, I get this... this dread. I start thinking I’m getting soft. Like, if I put on weight, I’ll be slower moving around cars at work, or up and down the court on the weekends. So yeah, the idea of gaining weight definitely scares me."
